1 Introduction 

## 1 Introduction 

This document is part three of the Conformance Test Specification (TR-03122). It is the counterpart of TR-03121-3 describing the test cases for the defined Function Modules. 

Not all Function Modules have test cases associated with them. 

Federal Office for Information Security 

1 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## 2 Test Cases Function Modules 

The following sections define test cases for Function Modules. 

## 2.1 Test Cases Acquisition Hardware 

## 2.1.1 Test Cases FM AH-ALL-SSS 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-ALL-SSS-001** 

- _Scope_ Examination of the surveillance camera systems of the self-service system (SSS) used for to obtain digitised facial images and fingerprints. 

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. **•** A surveillance camera system which supervises the area around the SSS is available. **•** A surveillance camera system which supervises the fingerprint capture system of the SSS is available. 

- **•** The IUT implements a graphical user interface. **•** The IUT implements a user interface of fingerprint acquisition shown to the biometric subject. **•** The following test resources are necessary: **•** Two test subjects are available to perform the acquisition with. 

- _CTS Mode_ **•** interactive **•** no provision of pre-defined input data 

   - `HTTP method: GET` 

   - test case variants: 

```
/TR03122/TC-AH-ALL-SSS-001/1
```

**Table 2.2** TC-AH-ALL-SSS-001 Test Case Variants 

_Description_ 

**Step Description / Expected Result** 1 _Description:_ 

- Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

- Perform a bona fide fingerprint presentation on the fingerprint scanner of the IUT. 

- Interact with the graphical user interface of the IUT. 

Federal Office for Information Security 

2 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-ALL-SSS-001** 

_Expected Result:_ 

   - The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

   - A fingerprint image is captured. 

   - The surveillance camera system captures an image of the surrounding area. 

   - The image of the surrounding area is made at the moment of the finger cap ture attempt (i.e. the timestamps of the corresponding images are less than 100ms apart). 

   - The image captured by the camera system surveying the environment has a resolution of at least 1280 x 720. 

   - The image of the surrounding area is a coloured image, in which the face of the biometric subject using the fingerprint scanner is visible. 

   - The surveillance camera system captures a colour image of the fingerprint acquisition area at the moment of the finger capture attempt. 

   - The image of the fp area is made at the moment of the finger capture at tempt (i.e. the timestamps of the corresponding images are less than 100ms apart). 

- The image captured by the camera system surveying the fp_area has a reso lution of at least 1280 x 720. 

- **•** The image of the fp area is a coloured image in which the fingerprint captu re system is shown up close. 

- **•** The images captured by the surveillance camera systems are cached locally. 

- 2 _Description:_ **•** A single biometric subject initiates the SSS process of the IUT. _Expected Result:_ **•** The IUT starts the SSS process and enables interaction via the graphical user interface. 

- 3 _Description:_ **•** A second biometric subject moves into the corridor of the SSS (distance of 100cm in front the SSS). 

- _Expected Result:_ **•** The image captured by the surveillance camera system of the surrounding area allows to identify whether that more than one person is in range of the SSS. 

- 4 _Description:_ **•** Both biometric subjects leave the corridor of the SSS. _Expected Result:_ 

   - The IUT stops the SSS process. 

**Table 2.1** Test Case ID: TC-AH-ALL-SSS-001

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-BCL-002** 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `//bio:FaceAcquisition` . Exactly 1 element is expected in ac cordance to the acquistion process. 

- The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `//bio:FaceAcquisition//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRe cord/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value/node()` . Exactly 1 element is ex pected in accordance to the acquistion process. 

- The image contained in //bio:FaceAcquisition//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRe cord/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value/node() has a resolution of at least 1080 x 1440. 

- The image contained in //bio:FaceAcquisition//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRe cord/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value/node() has a resolution of at most 1200 x 1600. 

- The image depicts the testperson (i.e. their fingers or face) in the moment of the acquisition. 

**Table 2.4** Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-BCL-002 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-BCL-003** 

_Scope_ Examination of image processing features of the digital camera or provided/integrated software used to obtain facial biometrics. 

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. 

   - The hardware used in the specific application scenario is connected 

   - The IUT implements an interface for conformance testing where a single image can be captured. 

   - The IUT is able to return the results for every captured image via the test interface. 

- The following test resources are necessary: **•** 

- _CTS Mode_ **•** interactive 

      - Two test subjects are available to perform the acquisition with. 

   - no provision of pre-defined input data 

   - `HTTP method: GET` 

   - test case variants: 

`/TR03122/TC-AH-FI-BCL-003/1` **Table 2.7** TC-AH-FI-BCL-003 Test Case Variants _Description_ **Step Description / Expected Result**

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-BCL-003** 

1 

_Description:_ 

- Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

- Position one biometric subject in front of the camera system. Verify that this biometric subject is standing in a distance above 40 cm and below 100 cm to the camera system. Note, the distance between camera system and biometric subject is defined as the geometrical optical-path length between the forehead of the biometric subject and the active camera system's op tic. The optical-path MAY, for example, follow a straight line from forehead to optic, or be rerouted by using mirrors so that the biometric subject can stand closer to the device 

- Position the second biometric subject in front of the camera system. Veri fy that the biometric subject is standing in a distance below 40 cm to the ca mera system. 

- Attempt to acquire an image with the camera system by using the CTS. 

- Examine the response returned by the IUT via the test interface. 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The response returned via the test interface ( `bio:FaceAcquisition` ) does not contain facial image data. 

**Table 2.6** Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-BCL-003

6 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-BCL-004** 

_Description:_ 

- 1 _Description:_ **•** Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

   - Position one biometric subject in front of the camera system. Verify that this biometric subject is standing in a distance above 40 cm and below 100 cm to the camera system. Note, the distance between camera system and biometric subject is defined as the geometrical optical-path length between the forehead of the biometric subject and the active camera system's op tic. The optical-path MAY, for example, follow a straight line from forehead to optic, or be rerouted by using mirrors so that the biometric subject can stand closer to the device. 

   - **•** Position the second biometric subject in front of the camera system. Verify that the biometric subject is standing in a distance above 100 cm to the ca mera system. 

   - Acquire an image with the camera system by using the CTS. 

   - Examine the response returned by the IUT via the test interface. 

   - _Expected Result:_ 

   - The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

   - A message conforming to TR-03121 XML containing the encoded facial image is returned via the test interface ( `bio:FaceAcquisition` ). 

   - The returned image depicts the biometric subject which was positioned in a distance above 40 cm and below 100 cm to the camera system. 

**Table 2.8** Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-BCL-004

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

**Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-DC2-003** 2 _Description:_ 

- Examination of the provided product documentation regarding effective focal length _feff_ . If the document does not explicitly specify the used effec tive focal length (compared to a 35 mm full frame), calculate the used effec tive focal length via _f_ e® = _f=cf_ where _f_ is the used focal length and _cf_ the crop factor (the crop factor may be calculated from the sensor's physical di mensions). If the camera model uses different lenses, additional evidence on which lense is actually being used shall be collected. Note: In some cases the effective focal length may also be extracted from the EXIF data. 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The used effective focal length _f_ e® = _f=cf_ is above 50 mm and below 130 mm. 

**Table 2.14** Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-DC2-003 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-DC2-004** 

- _Scope_ Examination of the image quality of the digital camera used to obtain facial biometrics. This test ca se covers the requirements for focal length (depending on the size of the camera sensor) as descri bed in [BIB_ICAO_TR_Portrait_Quality] (chapters 5.2.1 and 5.2.2). 

- _Applicability_ This TC applies to IUT which use a smartphone or a similar device instead of the DSLR or system camera originally intended by the FM. Note: This TC does not extend to digital cameras which only support a wide-angle lens (e.g. webcams). 

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. **•** Product documentation of the camera model provides information regarding permitted came ra-to-subject distances and zoom settings. 

- **•** The IUT implements an interface for conformance testing where a single image of the target (cf. [BIB_ICAO_TR_Portrait_Quality]) can be captured. 

- **•** The target is lit evenly from the front. A strong lightsource (i.e. softbox) is placed approximately 35° atop of the imaginary line between camera and target. 

- **•** The following test resources are necessary: **•** A conformant test-target. **•** An image-processing programm which allows to rotate a picture and crop it without quality loss. 

- **•** A script which allows to measure the size of a square in a picture. **•** Product documentation of IUT is at hand (e.g. data sheet, manual). 

- _CTS Mode_ **•** interactive **•** no provision of pre-defined input data **•** `HTTP method: GET` **•** test case variants: `/TR03122/TC-AH-FI-DC2-004/1` 

- **Table 2.16** TC-AH-FI-DC2-004 Test Case Variants 

- _Description_ **Step Description / Expected Result** 

Federal Office for Information Security 

10 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-DC2-004** 

_Description:_ 

- 1 _Description:_ **•** The target is positioned directly in front of the camera at the desired working distance (cf. Evaluation Step). Camera and target are at the same height. 

- **•** The IUT including camera system is configured according to the documen tation. 

- **•** Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. **•** A single image of the target is acquired. _Expected Result:_ **•** The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- **•** The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `/bio:FaceAcquisition/bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecor d[@type="BMP" or @type="JPEG"]/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Va lue/node()` . Exactly 1 element is expected in accordance to the acquistion process. 

- 2 _Description:_ **•** Examine the response returned by the IUT via the test interface. _Expected Result:_ **•** The image depicts the target. **•** In all depicted squares the 0.5 mm lines shall be distinguishable. **•** The depicted straight lines of the target (especially the lines further away from the center) shall appear straight to a human observer. The squares on the target shall be depicted with right angles to a human observer. No barrel distortion is visible. 

- 3 _Description:_ **•** The acquired images are prepared in a suitable way using the image proces sing program: If necessary, the images may be rotated slightly to appear ver tically upright in the picture. 

   - The acquired image is horizontally divided into four lines of three pictures containing a single pattern s.t. each of the patterns is depicted. Each line is then divided into two groups of images: One group containing the leftmost and center pattern, the other containing the center and rightmost pattern. 

   - For each of the groups: measure the magnification distortion, i.e. the diffe rences in height of the left/rightmost pattern in comparison to the center pattern. Note: To ensure comparability between different measurements a script shall be used for support. 

   - _Expected Result:_ 

|||_Expected Result:_|
|---|---|---|
|||**•**<br>The average radiation distortion on the left and on the right are below 5%.|
|_Evaluation_|**Evaluation Step**|**Description / Evaluation Result**|
||1|_Description:_|
|||**•**<br>If the product documentation permits several different CSD or zoom set|
|||tings, the TC shall be repeated for all of them. Alternatively, if this is not|
|||practicable, the TC shall be repeated for each combination of the respective|
|||minimum, medium and maximum values.|
|||_Expected Result:_|



- For each combination of CSD and zoom setting the expected results are met. 

**Table 2.15** Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-DC2-004

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-EGT-001** 

|_Scope_|Examination of technical specifications of the camera system used to obtain digitised facial images|Examination of technical specifications of the camera system used to obtain digitised facial images|Examination of technical specifications of the camera system used to obtain digitised facial images|
|---|---|---|---|
||in|an|e-gate scenario.|
|_Preconditions_|**•**|The following test resources are necessary:||
|||**•**|Product documentation of IUT is at hand (e.g. data sheet, manual).|
|||**•**|Product documentation of camera model is at hand (e.g. data sheet, manual).|
|_CTS Mode_|Not supported|||
|_Description_|**Step**||**Description / Expected Result**|
||1||_Description:_|
||||**•**<br>Consult product documentation of the camera system.|
||||_Expected Result:_|
||||**•**<br>The product documentation states the support of capturing facial images|
||||of a biometric subject with a body height in the range of 140 cm to 200 cm|
||||standing in front of the camera system.|
||||**•**<br>The product documentation states a physical resolution of at least 600x800|
||||pixels without application of upscaling.|

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-EGT-002** 

1 

_Description:_ 

- Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

- Position the biometric subject in a distance of 50 cm towards the camera system. 

- Acquire an image with the camera system. 

- Examine the response returned by the IUT via the test interface. 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `//bio:FaceAcquisition` . Exactly 1 element is expected in ac cordance to the acquistion process. 

- The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `.//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecord/tradd:BinaryDa ta/tradd:Value` . Exactly 1 element is expected in accordance to the ac quistion process. 

- The image returned by the IUT has a resolution of at least 600 x 800. 

- The image has sufficient sharpness for visual identification of the subject and does not obviously distort the captured face. 

2

- Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

- Position the biometric subject in a distance of 100 cm towards the camera system.

- The image returned by the IUT has a resolution of at least 600 x 800. 

- The image has sufficient sharpness for visual identification of the subject and does not obviously distort the captured face. 

3

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FI-SSS2-004** 

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. 

   - The integrated camera system used in the specific application scenario is connected 

   - The IUT implements an interface for conformance testing where a single image can be captured. 

   - **•** The IUT is able to return the results for every captured image via the test interface. 

   - The following test resources are necessary: 

      - One test subject is available to perform the acquisition with. 

      - **•** Product documentation of IUT is at hand (e.g. data sheet, manual). 

- _CTS Mode_ **•** interactive 

   - no provision of pre-defined input data 

   - `HTTP method: GET` 

   - test case variants: 

```
/TR03122/TC-AH-FI-SSS2-004/1
```

**Table 2.37** TC-AH-FI-SSS2-004 Test Case Variants 

|_Description_|**Step**|**Description / Expected Result**|
|---|---|---|
||1|_Description:_|
|||**•**<br>Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface.|



- Position the biometric subject looking frontally into the camera at a distan ce of 70 cm from the camera system. This distance is to be understood as the length of the optical path. 

- Acquire a facial image with the camera system. 

- Examine the response returned by the IUT via the test interface. 

- _Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `//bio:FaceAcquisition/bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecor d[@type='bmp' or @type='png' or @type='jpeg' or @type='jpe g2000']/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value/node()` . Exactly 1 element is expected in accordance to the acquistion process. 

- The image captured by the camera has a resolution of at least 1244 x 1600. 

- The image is a coloured image with a colour space of 24 bit sRGB. 

- The response contains a facial image according to the definition of "full frontal" (see [ISO_FACE]). 

2 _Description:_ 

- Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

- Position the biometric subject looking frontally into the camera at a distan ce of 100 cm from the camera system. This distance is to be understood as the length of the optical path. 

- Acquire a facial image with the camera system. 

- Examine the response returned by the IUT via the test interface.

||Method A: It is possible to control the integration time of the scanner’s image sensor.|
||Method B: It is possible to control the intensity of the scanner’s illumination.|
||Method C: It is only possible to turn the scanner’s illumination on and off.|
||Method D: Neither of the above can be controlled.|
|_Preconditions_|**•**<br>The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded.|
||**•**<br>The scanner is connected.|
||**•**<br>The scanner software is set to output unprocessed images.|
||**•**<br>Captured images can be saved for subsequent evaluation.|
||**•**<br>The following test resources are necessary:|
||**•**<br>Method A and B: A diffuse reflecting uniform white target (e.g. Munsell N9).|
||**•**<br>Method C: An external extended uniform light source (e.g. LED backlight or similar).|
||**•**<br>Method B and C: Measurement equipment for measuring the luminance of the illumination.|
||**•**<br>Method D: A diffuse reflecting target with a grey scale step tablet or a set of uniform neutral|
||grey targets cards with various known reflectance.|
||**•**<br>Image processing software to determine the average grey value in a region of interest in the|
||image.|
|_CTS Mode_|Not supported|
|_Description_|**Step**<br>**Description / Expected Result**|

24 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FP-OPT-001** 

_Description:_ 

- 1 _Description:_ **•** Method A: The white target is placed on the scanner platen. The scanner’s il lumination is turned on and left unchanged during the test. Multiple images are taken while varying the integration time of the image sensor. The set ting of the integration time is recorded with each image. 

- **•** Method B: The white target is placed on the scanner platen. Multiple images are taken while varying the intensity of the illumination of the scanner. The luminance of the illumination is monitored at the same time and recorded with each image. 

- **•** Method C: The scanner’s illumination is turned off. The external light sour ce is placed on the scanner platen. Multiple images are taken while varying the intensity of the external illumination. The luminance of the external il lumination is monitored at the same time and recorded with each image. 

- **•** Method D: The grey scale target is placed on the scanner platen and an image is taken. In case of a set of uniform targets images of each target are taken sequentially and the reflectance of the target is recorded with each image. 

- **•** All methods: At least nine images/individual grey values are required that substantially cover the scanners dynamic range. 

- _Expected Result:_ **•** Set of images with a uniform area covered by the target or a single image of a target with a grey scale step tablet. 

- 2 _Description:_ **•** For each of the images the average grey value is determined in the area co vered by the colour test card. The average grey value is plotted against the previously recorded integration time (Method A), luminance (Method B, C) or reflectance (Method D). 

- _Expected Result:_ **•** The resulting plot should be close to a linear function. **•** A linear regression is performed over the value pairs (integration time, aver age grey value),(luminance, average grey value) or (reflectance, grey value). The difference between the average grey value and the grey value from re gression is calculated for each measurement. 

- **•** None of the calculated differences exceed 7.65 grey levels. 

- 3 _Description:_ 

   - Method A: The white target is placed on the scanner platen. The scanner’s il lumination is turned on and left unchanged during the test. Multiple images are taken while varying the integration time of the image sensor. The set ting of the integration time is recorded with each image. 

   - Method B: The white target is placed on the scanner platen. Multiple images are taken while varying the intensity of the illumination of the scanner. The luminance of the illumination is monitored at the same time and recorded with each image. 

   - A linear regression is performed over the value pairs (integration time, aver age grey value),(luminance, average grey value) or (reflectance, grey value). The difference between the average grey value and the grey value from re gression is calculated for each measurement. 

   - _Expected Result:_ 

   - None of the calculated differences exceed 7.65 grey levels.

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FP-OPT-002** 

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. 

   - The scanner is connected. 

   - The scanner software is set to output unprocessed images. 

   - Captured images can be saved for subsequent evaluation. 

   - The following test resources are necessary: 

      - A Ronchi bar pattern target, that consists of an equal-width bar and space square wave pat tern at 1.0 cy/mm, preferably on a flexible, diffuse white reflecting substrate. The target pat tern shall cover at least 70% of the scanner’s capture area. 

      - Test software.

|---|---|---|

||**•**|The target is placed on the scanner platen and images are taken with the li|
|||nes of the Ronchi pattern parallel to the pixel columns (vertical bars) and|
|||rows (horizontal bars) of the image sensor, respectively. A flexible target has|
|||to be pressed against the scanner platen to make sure the target is in con|
|||tact with the platen over the entire pattern area. To discern properties of the|
|||scanner and the target the procedure may be repeated with the target being|

|||location they are most probably due to the scanner.|

- The obtained image files are converted to raw image data (pixel grey values without header) and are processed with the software WinGeo. The program calculates the 1-bar and 6-bar distances of the pattern in the image (acrossbar accuracy) as well as the along-bar accuracy. Also the resolution is calcu lated from the 6-bar distances. 

_Expected Result:_ 

- Resolution: The scanner’s output resolution in both sensor detector row and column directions is ( _R §_ 0 _:_ 01 _R_ ), with _R_ being 500 pixels per inch or 1000 pixels per inch. 

- If a 500-ppi scanner is tested: Across-bar accuracy: The difference _D_ = _jY ¡ Xj_ between the distance of the bars on the target ( _X_ ) and the cor responding distance measured in the image ( _Y_ ) does not exceed in at least 99% of the tested cases in horizontal and vertical direction, respectively: _D ·_ 0 _:_ 0007 for 0 _:_ 00 _< X ·_ 0 _:_ 07 and _D ·_ 0 _:_ 01 _X_ for 0 _:_ 07 _· X ·_ 1 _:_ 5. _D_ , _X_ , _Y_ are in inches.

- Along-bar accuracy: In both vertical bar and horizontal bar orientations, the maximum difference in the horizontal or vertical direction, respectively, between the locations of any two points within a 1.5-inch segment of a gi ven bar image does not exceed 0.016 inches for at least 99.0 percent of the tested cases.

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FP-OPT-003** 

_Scope_ Test of spatial frequency response. 

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. 

   - The scanner is connected. 

   - The scanner software is set to output unprocessed images. 

   - Captured images can be saved for evaluation. 

   - The following test resources are necessary: 

      - A test chart containing Ronchi test patterns meeting or exceeding the minimum require ments given in Table 2.42 for 500 ppi scanners or in Table 2.43 for 1000 ppi scanners. The target must also contain large black and white patches for normalizing the contrast. Prefera bly the target should consist of dark patterns on a flexible, diffuse white reflecting substrate material. The material shall be optically opaque and exhibit no significant volume scattering as this degrades the contrast of the patterns. Rigid materials should not be used, if possible, to avoid damage to the image sensor. 

      - A file describing the type, position and size of the different patterns on the test chart. This is required for processing the captured images with WinMTF. 

      - Test software.

|---|---|
|_CTS Mode_|Not supported|
|_Description_|**Step**|



|**Step**|**Description / Expected Result**|**Description / Expected Result**|
|---|---|---|
|1|_Description:_||
||**•**|The target is placed on the scanner platen and images are taken with the li|
|||nes of the Ronchi pattern parallel to the pixel columns (vertical bars) and|
|||rows (horizontal bars) of the image sensor, respectively. A flexible target has|
|||to be pressed against the scanner platen to make sure the target is in con|
|||tact with the platen over the entire pattern area. If the target pattern covers|
|||less than 25% of the scanners capture area, the procedure is applied to the|
|||right and the left half of the capture area, respectively. The bars of the Ron|
|||chi patterns have to be aligned with the pixel rows and columns to an ang|
|||le less than 0.5°. If the images from the scanner are inverted they have to be|
|||inverted back to show dark patterns on a bright background.|
||_Expected Result:_||
||**•**|Set of images with the bars of the Ronchi pattern in vertical and horizontal|
|||direction, showing dark patterns on a bright background.|
|2|_Description:_||



- The resulting images files are converted to TIFF format and are processed with the tool WinMTF. This program determines the modulation in each of the Ronchi test patterns. These modulation values are absolute with respect to the dynamic range of the image and do not represent the contrast trans fer function (CTF). 

_Expected Result:_ 

- Absolute modulation values for each of the Ronchi patterns on the test chart. 

3 _Description:_ 

- The absolute modulation values have to be normalized by the modulation of the large black and white patches on the test chart, representing a spati al frequency of approximately zero cycles per millimetre. The average grey level in the black and white patches is calculated and the modulation is cal culated as follows: _M_ 0 = _[G][white][ ¡][ G][black]_ with Modulation for 0 cy/mm ( _M_ 0), _Gwhite_ + _Gblack_ 

- average grey level in the white patch( _Gwhite_ ), and average grey level in the black patch ( _Gblack_ ). Having determined _M_ 0 the value of the CTF can be cal culated from the modulation of the Ronchi pattern _CTF_ ( _R_ ) = _[M]_[(] _[R]_[))] _M_ 0 

- with _M_ ( _R_ ) Modulation at spatial frequency _R_ .

## **Test Case ID: TC-AH-FP-OPT-004** 

_Scope_ Test of signal to noise ratio and grey level uniformity. _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. 

- The scanner is connected. 

- The scanner software is set to output unprocessed images. 

- Captured images can be saved for evaluation. 

- The following test resources are necessary: 

   - A uniform diffuse reflecting neutral grey target of high reflectance that covers the scanner’s entire capture area (e.g Munsell N9). 

   - A uniform diffuse reflecting neutral grey target of low reflectance that covers the scanner’s entire capture area (e.g Munsell N2). 

   - Test software. 

||**•**<br>Test soft|
|---|---|
|_CTS Mode_|Not supported|
|_Description_|**Step**|



|**Step**|**Description / Expected Result**|**Description / Expected Result**|
|---|---|---|
|1|_Description:_||
||**•**|The black target is placed on the scanner platen and an image is taken. After|
|||that the procedure is repeated with the white target. The definition of “black|
|||image” and “white image” for the subsequent analyses depends on the grey|
|||value that the target would produce in the final fingerprint image. If high|
|||reflectance produces a high grey value in the final fingerprint image then|
|||the image obtained with the high reflectance target is considered the “white|
|||image”. If low reflectance produces a high grey value (inverting behaviour)|
|||then the image obtained with the low reflectance target is considered the|
|||“white image”. The other image is then considered the “black image”.|
||_Expected Result:_||
||**•**|A black image with the average grey value in the image being at least 4 grey|
|||levels above the scanner’s minimum output grey level.|
||**•**|A white image with the average grey value in the image being at least 4 grey|
|||levels below the scanner’s maximum output grey level.|
|2|_Description:_||



- The black and white images are converted to raw image data (pixel grey va lues without header) and are processed with the software signal-to-noise ra tio (SNR). The program divides the images in blocks of 6.35 mm x 6.35 mm and determines the average grey level, the standard deviation, the grey le vel averages in the columns and rows, respectively, and the number of pi xels with a higher-than-allowed difference of the grey level from the aver age in each of the blocks. With these quantities the required tests regarding signal-noise-ratio and grey level uniformity are performed.

_Expected Result:_ 

|**•**|Signal to noise ratio: Signal is defined as the difference between the aver<br>age grey levels of corresponding blocks in the white and the black image.<br>Noise is defined as the standard deviation of the grey levels in each block<br>of the white and black image, respectively. The signal-noise ratio is cal<br>culated as_SNRblack_ =<br>_Gwhite ¡_<br>_Gblack_<br>_¾black_<br>_; SNRwhite_=<br>_Gwhite ¡_<br>_Gblack_<br>_¾white_<br>with<br>_Gwhite;_<br>_Gblack_average grey level in a block of the black and white image, re<br>spectively and_¾white; ¾black_standard deviation of the grey levels in a block<br>of the black and white image, respectively. The signal to noise ratio exceeds<br>125 for at least 97% of the tested blocks in both the black and the white<br>image.|
|---|---|



- Grey level uniformity requirement #1 (adjacent row, column uniformity): At least 99% of the average grey levels between every two adjacent 6.35 mm long rows and 99% between every two adjacent 6.35 mm long columns, do not differ by more than 1.0 grey levels in the black and do not differ by mo re than 2.0 grey levels in the white image. 

- Grey level uniformity requirement #2 (pixel to pixel uniformity): For at least 99.9% of all pixels within every independent 6.35 mm x 6.35 mm block no individual pixel’s grey level varies from the average by more than 8.0 grey levels in the black image, and does not vary from the average by more than 22.0 grey levels in the white image. 

- **•** Grey level uniformity requirement #3 (small area uniformity): For every two independent 6.35 mm x 6.35 mm blocks the average grey levels of the two blocks do not differ by more than 3.0 grey levels in the black image, and do not differ by more than 12.0 grey levels in the white image.

Federal Office for Information Security 

33 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AS-FI-DC2-001** 

1 

_Description:_ 

- Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

- Capture an image with the camera. 

- Examine encoding, format and resolution of the returned digitised image data. 

- Check the resulting image for visible compression artefacts. 

- Check that software-based background elimination and software-based background replacement have not been performed. 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecord[@type='bmp' or @ty pe='png' or @type='jpeg' or @type='jpeg2000' and not(@copy Reasons)]/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value/node()` . Exactly 1 element is expected in accordance to the acquistion process. 

- The data embedded in the element `//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRe cord/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value` of the response XML is base64-en coded data that can be successfully decoded resulting in an image file for mat according to the corresponding @type-attribute. 

- The image captured by the camera has a resolution of at least 1244 x 1600. 

- The image has a colour space of 24bit RGB. 

- If the image is a JPEG or lossily compressed JPEG2000 image: The acquisiti on device does not support a lossless mode and the image is provided with the least compression possible. 

- The image does not contain any visible compression artefacts. 

- The image depicts the testperson in the moment of the acquisition. 

- The image has the same background as in the moment of the acquisition. 

**Table 2.50** Test Case ID: TC-AS-FI-DC2-001

**Test Case ID: TC-AS-FI-ICS2-008** 

_Scope_ Examination of the software module used for acquisition of digitised facial images with focus on the background elimination. _Applicability_ This TC only applies to IUT which perform a software-based segmentation. 

Federal Office for Information Security 

40 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AS-FI-ICS2-008** 

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. 

   - The groundtruth database is at hand. 

   - The facial images in the database are either 24bit RGB or 8bit grayscale. 

   - The following metrics shall be used to calculate the performance of the image segmentation: In tersection over Union and Hausdorff Distance. 

   - The following conditions apply regarding the database: 

      - The image data that is sent to the IUT is of the following biometric type: FACE. 

      - The groundtruth database contains pairs of images in format BMP. 

      - Each pair consists of a facial image with non-uniform background and an image with the seg mentation result, i.e. an image of the same dimensions as the facial image with pixels of eit her red, black or white. A pixel in the segmentation result is black iff the corresponding pi xel in the facial image is a background-pixel, otherwise it is red or white. Iff the pixel is part of the critical "core region" of the face (i.e. the area of the face containing mouth, eyes, nose) it is red. Otherwise, the pixels are white. 

      - For each image the groundtruth-db contains dedicated thresholds for each of the metrics. 

      - The images in the database have dimensions of 1600*1244. 

      - The facial images in the database are either 24bit RGB or 8bit grayscale. 

_CTS Mode_ 

- automatic 

- The image data that is sent to the IUT may have the following format: 

   - BMP 

- `HTTP method: POST` 

- test case variants: 

## `/TR03122/TC-AS-FI-ICS2-008/1` 

**Table 2.67** TC-AS-FI-ICS2-008 Test Case Variants 

_Description_ 

**Step Description / Expected Result** 1 _Description:_ 

- The IUT is called via the conformance test interface and an image from the database is provided. The image meets the properties described in the pre conditions. 

- Calculate the Hausdorff distance. 

- Calculate the IoU-score. 

Federal Office for Information Security 

41 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AS-FI-ICS2-008** 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `.//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecord[@type='bmp']` . Exactly 2 elements are expected in accordance to the acquistion process. 

- The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `.//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecord[@type='bmp' and @externalReference='facialImage']` . Exactly 1 element is expected in accordance to the acquistion process. 

- The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `.//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecord[@type='bmp' and @externalReference='segmentationPrediction']` . Exactly 1 element is expected in accordance to the acquistion process. 

- The data embedded in the elements ".//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecord[@ty pe='bmp']" of the response XML are BMP. 

- The image containing the facial image with eliminated background has a re solution of 1244 x 1600. 

- The image containing the segmentation prediction has a resolution of 1244 x 1600. 

- The calculated Hausdorff Distance is below the threshold from the ground truth database. 

- The calculated IoU is below the threshold from the groundtruth database. 

- The pixels of facial image demarked as "non-background" by the segmenta tion predicition image are identical to the "non-background" pixels of the corresponding request image. 

- The pixels of facial image demarked as "background" by the segmentation predicition image are of gray color in between #A1A1A1 and #E1E1E1. 

|||**•**<br>The pixels of facial image demarked as "background" by the segmentation<br>predicition image are of gray color in between #A1A1A1 and #E1E1E1.|
|---|---|---|

|||the segmentation result) is classfied as a background pixel by the IUT (i.e. a|

## **Test Case ID: TC-AS-FI-ICS2-009** 

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. 

   - A reference implementation for background replacement is at hand. 

   - The IUT is configured such that for every captured image two versions of the captured image are released: One of the images is an original presentation of the biometric subject during the captu re, the other one is the result of the application of the background elimination feature. 

- It is possible to perform this testcase under different conditions including: cluttered Back ground, uniform background in a distinct color (e.g. blue or green), subject with short hair (ear length or tied back), subject with long hair (not tied back), subject wearing glasses, subject wea ring patterned clothes 

- _CTS Mode_ **•** interactive 

   - no provision of pre-defined input data 

   - `HTTP method: GET` 

   - test case variants: 

```
/TR03122/TC-AS-FI-ICS2-009/1
```

**Table 2.69** TC-AS-FI-ICS2-009 Test Case Variants 

|_Description_|**Step**|**Description / Expected Result**|
|---|---|---|
||1|_Description:_|



- Caprture a facial image of a biometric subject using the IUT in order to test the segmentation. 

- _Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- **•** The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `.//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecord[@type='jpeg']` . Exactly 2 elements are expected in accordance to the acquistion process. 

- The image in `.//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecord[@type='jpeg' and @externalReference='segmentedImage']` in the response is the segmen ted image, i.e. an image of the same dimensions as the facial image with grey pixels. A pixel in the segmented image is grey if the corresponding pixel in the facial image is a background-pixel. 

- The image in `.//bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecord[@type='jpeg' and @externalReference='originalImage']` in the response is the non-seg mented image, i.e. a facial image without any added grey pixels. This is the only difference between the images (i.e. there is no difference regarding cropping, rotation, colorgrading etc.). 

- The image containing the facial image with background has a resolution of 1244 x 1600. 

- The image containing the facial image with eliminated background has a re solution of 1244 x 1600. 

- The pixels of facial image demarked as "background" and replaced by the IUT image are of the same gray color in between #A1A1A1 and #E1E1E1. It is allowed to blur the transition between original and segmented image. 

- The noise of the image is less than 0.5/FF. 

2 

_Description:_ 

- Evaluate the segmented image for changes in the core facial region, i.e. the triangular region including eyes, nose and mouth; glasses are included. 

- Evaluate the segmented image for changes in the outline. This includes the head, shoulders, clothes and accesoires (i.e. earrings). 

Federal Office for Information Security 

43 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AS-FI-ICS2-009** 

_Expected Result:_ 

      - No change is made in the core region. This includes individual pixels. 

   - No area of at least 3px x 3px in the foreground is removed or blurred. This includes for example the removal of an accesoire, removal of parts of the shoulder or the head. The removal of hair does not alter the shape or hair style (for example by removing asymmetric parts). 

   - 3 _Description:_ **•** Use the reference implementation to remove the background in the original image. 

      - Calculate the IoU and Hausdorff distance between the background replaced images from the IUT and the reference implementation. 

      - _Expected Result:_ 

      - The IoU in between the IUT response and the reference implementation is less than 10%. 

      - The Hausdorff distance in between the IUT response and the reference im plementation is less than 10. 

      - **•** No background object bigger than 3px x 3px is visible or reconstructable in the image. 

- Note: The difference in between the IUT response and the reference imple mentation may be greater in case the IUT response performs better than the reference implementation according to a check as per the evauation step. 

- _Evaluation_ **Evaluation Step Description / Evaluation Result** 1 _Description:_ **•** Repeat this testcase 5 times under different conditions including: clutte red Background, uniform background in a distinct color (e.g. blue or green), subject with short hair (earlength or tied back), subject with long hair (not tied back), subject wearing glasses, subject wearing patterned clothes 

      - In case the automatic evaluation of step 3 fails, the IUT reponse may be eva luated as better than the reference implementation according to the follo wing conditions: 

      - _Expected Result:_ 

      - No part of the core facial region is eliminated. 

      - Hair, clothes and accessoires are kept coherently. 

      - No broader (> 3px x 3px) part of the hair, clothes or accessoires is removed or blurred. This includes objects which are located behind the ear or with considerable distance to the facial region (e.g. part of the hairstyle, collar, earrings). 

      - Singular small objects (e.g. individual hairs or fringes) may be altered. 

      - Small gaps (< 3px x 3px) between hairs or between glasses and face do not have to be eliminated. 

      - No object located in the background is kept as a whole. 

      - The transition between foreground and background may be kept (distance 5px). 

      - Blurred regions count as altered/eliminated.

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AS-FP-ROLL-001** 

1 

_Description:_ 

   - Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

   - Perform the flat acquisition process of all fingers in order to obtain the ne cessary reference data for the control verification. Ensure sufficient quality and correct execution throughout the process. 

   - For each finger of the test person: Perform a rolled fingerprint acquisition, rolling the finger uniformly from nail to nail. It is not required to acquire the fingerprints of all test persons directly in line. 

   - Examine the response returned by the IUT via the test interface. 

   - _Expected Result:_ 

   - The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

   - The response contains the encoded "flat" fingerprint images. 

   - The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `//bio:FingerAcquisition[bio:FingerCaptureMode = "roll"]/bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecord/tradd:BinaryData/trad d:Value/node()` . Exactly 10 elements are expected in accordance to the ac quistion process. 

   - The resulting image shows the full width of the rolled fingerprint. Typically, the image will roughly be of rectangular shape. 

   - The captured fingerprint image does not depict visible distortion or inter ruptions. The captured fingerprint image depicts the fingerprint from nail to nail. 

   - The captured fingerprint image depicts a faithful reproduction of the fin gerprint, especially in the areas where the rolled fingerprint overlaps with the corresponding plain print. 

   - The captured fingerprint image does not depict puzzle effects such that parts of the fingerprint image are displaced from their actual position 

   - The captured fingerprint image clearly depicts friction ridges. 

   - The captured fingerprint image does not depict blurring and smearing. 

   - The captured fingerprint image clearly depicts ridge patterns. 

   - If features exists for the given fingerprint: The captured fingerprint image clearly depicts features. 

   - If loop features exists for the given fingerprint: The captured fingerprint image clearly depicts loop features (core and delta). 

   - The captured fingerprint image clearly depicts existing features at the bor der zone of the image. 

   - The captured fingerprint image depicts the fingerprint's upper part. 

   - The captured fingerprint image depicts the fingerprint’s core area with ridge lines 

   - If delta features exists for the given fingerprint: The captured fingerprint image depicts the fingerprints delta features. 

   - The captured fingerprint image depicts the fingerprint’s baseline (bottom area). 

- The vertical axis of the fingerprint depicted in the captured image is in par allel with the fingerprint image’s vertical axis. 

- _Evaluation_ **Evaluation Step Description / Evaluation Result** 

Federal Office for Information Security 

50 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-AS-FP-ROLL-001** 

1 

_Description:_ 

- The TC shall be repeated for each test person. It is required to acquire all ten fingerprints of different test persons and not from one test person several times to achieve five capture processes. 

- For each capture process: Check the Expected Results. In case of doubt whe ther one or several of the expected results hold true for the given finger print, consult a dactyloscopy expert for clarification. 

- _Expected Result:_ 

- The fingerprints resulting from each of the five capture process meet the expectations formulated above.

## **Test Case ID: TC-AS-FP-SLP-002** 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The documentation gives information of the performance of the algorithm based on a reference database. 

- The documentation gives evidence that the classification algorithm ensures an accuracy of at least 99%, i.e. 99% of left and right hand slaps are correctly classified. 

- The documentation gives evidence that the classification algorithm can be calibrated for accuracy levels using configurable thresholds in case the clas sification algorithm returns more than two possible results, e.g. "left", "right" and "unknown".

## 2.3 Test Cases Biometric Image Processing 

## 2.3.1 Test Cases FM BIP-FI-APP 

## **Test Case ID: TC-BIP-FI-APP-001** 

_Scope_ 

Verify correct facial image processing with respect to the output of integrated camera systems to obtain a facial image. The metrics applied for assessment are based on [ISO_FACE] . 

Federal Office for Information Security 

57 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-BIP-FI-APP-001** 

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. 

   - The IUT implements an interface for conformance testing where a single image can be provided and the according biometric container including the image data is returned. 

   - The following definitions according to [ISO_FACE] are relevant for verifying the geometric re quirements: The intersection of the vertical line passing through the approximate horizontal midpoints of the mouth and the bridge of the nose, and the horizontal line connecting the cen tres of the two eyes, is called the centre of the face. The width of the head is defined as the dis tance between the two imaginary lines parallel to the vertical line mentioned above. Each line is drawn between the upper and lower lobes of each ear and positioned where the external ear connects the head. The length of the head is defined as the distance between the base of the chin and the crown (i.e. the top of the head ignoring any hair) measured on the vertical line mentio ned above. 

   - The following conditions apply regarding the database: 

      - The image data that is sent to the IUT is of the following biometric type: FACE. 

      - The groundtruth database contains noncompliant images w.r.t. the required quality values. 

      - The database contains images, that are not cropped to the face. 

- _CTS Mode_ **•** automatic 

   - The image data that is sent to the IUT may have the following format: 

      - JPEG 

   - `HTTP method: POST` 

   - test case variants: 

## `/TR03122/TC-BIP-FI-APP-001/1` 

**Table 2.92** TC-BIP-FI-APP-001 Test Case Variants 

_Description_ 

|**Step**|**Description / Expected Result**|
|---|---|
|1|_Description:_|



- The IUT is called via the conformance test interface and an image from the database is provided. The image meets the properties described in the pre conditions. 

- Check correct processing of the output of integrated camera systems. 

- Determine the width of the processed image and the head width. 

- Determine the height of the processed image and the head length. 

- Determine the X and Y coordinates of the centre of the face, assuming that the origin of the coordinate system is in the upper left corner of the image. 

Federal Office for Information Security 

58 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-BIP-FI-APP-001** 

_Expected Result:_ 

   - The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

   - The response contains a single facial image. 

   - If the facial image is used in a different scenario than verification: The co lour space is sRGB. 

   - If the facial image is used in a different scenario than verification: The co lour depth is 24 bit. 

   - If the facial image is used in a verification scenario: The colour space is sRGB or grey scale is used. 

   - If the facial image is used in a verification scenario: The colour depth is 24 bit or a colour depth of 8 bit with grey scale used. 

   - The face is fully visible in foreground. 

   - The minimum distance between both eyes is 120 pixels. 

   - The processed image is cropped and de-rotated. 

   - The position of the face within the processed image meets the geometric re quirements of [ISO_FACE] , chapter 8.3, i.e.: The X coordinate of the centre of the face is between 45 % and 55 % of the image width. 

   - The position of the face within the processed image meets the geometric re quirements of [ISO_FACE] , chapter 8.3, i.e.: The Y coordinate of the centre of the face is between 30 % and 50 % (for children under the age of 11 bet ween 30 % and 60 %) of the image height. 

- The size of the face within the processed image meets the geometric requi rements of [ISO_FACE] , chapter 8.3, i.e.: The head width is between 50 % and 75 % of the image width. 

- **•** The size of the face within the processed image meets the geometric requi rements of [ISO_FACE] , chapter 8.3, i.e.: The head length is between 60 % and 90 % (for children under the age of 11 between 50 % and 90 %) of the vertical length of the image. 

- _Evaluation_ **Evaluation Step Description / Evaluation Result** 1 _Description:_ **•** Examine the response returned by the IUT via the test interface. _Expected Result:_ 

   - Overall, less then 0 percent of the requested images are not handled as ex pected. 

**Table 2.91** Test Case ID: TC-BIP-FI-APP-001

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded.

If producing GSAT3 data is in scope of the considered Application Profile the IUT SHALL provide both output types in parallel (generic facial image, facial image for German Standard for AFIS Transactions Version 3 (GSAT3) transaction). 

   - The following conditions apply regarding the database:

      - The groundtruth database contains noncompliant images w.r.t. the required quality values.

   - The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

   - The response contains a single facial image (outside a possibly existing GSAT3 XML).

## **Test Case ID: TC-BIP-FI-FBS-001** 

_Scope_ Verify correct image processing of application forms and the cropping of the facial image on the application form using a flat bad scanner. 

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. **•** The flat bed scanner used in the specific application scenario is connected 

- The IUT is able to return the results for every captured image via the test interface. 

- **•** The following test resources are necessary: **•** As test resource: an application form with a facial image that fulfills the requirements of "full frontal" specified in [ISO_FACE]. 

- **•** No provision of pre-defined input data. 

- _CTS Mode_ **•** interactive **•** no provision of pre-defined input data 

   - `HTTP method: GET` 

- test case variants: `/TR03122/TC-BIP-FI-FBS-001/1` 

- **Table 2.96** TC-BIP-FI-FBS-001 Test Case Variants 

- _Description_ **Step Description / Expected Result** 1 _Description:_ **•** Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

      - Position the application form and the image properly on the FBS. 

      - **•** Perform the scanning process. 

      - Examine the response returned by the IUT via the test interface. 

      - Examine the image contained in the response regarding crop, width, height and colour depth and colour space. 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `//bio:FaceDelivery/bio:Records/bio:BinaryRecor d[contains(@copyReasons, "cropped_automatically") or conta ins(@copyReasons, "rotated_automatically") or not(@copyRea sons)]/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value/node()` . Exactly 1 element is expected in accordance to the acquistion process. 

- The image image contained in the response has a resolution of 622 x 800. 

- The image is a coloured image with a colour space of 8 bit (greyscale) or 24 bit sRGB. 

- The processed image is cropped and de-rotated. 

- The face is centered and fully visible in the foreground. 

- The image depicts the testperson (i.e. their fingers or face) in the moment of the acquisition.

## 2.3.5 Test Cases FM BIP-FP-APP 

The acquisition of fingerprints can be performed with the help of a single fingerprint sensor or a multi finger print sensor. In the first case TC-BIP-FP-APP-001 has to be applied, in the second case TC-BIP-FP-APP-002. Furthermore, in the case of four finger sensors TC-BIP-FP-APP-003 has to applied additionally. 

- **Test Case ID: TC-BIP-FP-APP-001** _Scope_ Examination of segmentation in connection with single finger scanners. _Applicability_ This TC only applies when single finger sensors are being used. _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. **•** The single fingerprint scanner used in the specific application scenario is connected **•** The IUT implements an interface for conformance testing where biometric data can be acqui red. 

- **•** The following test resources are necessary: **•** A test person to perform the biometric acquisition with. **•** No provision of pre-defined input data. 

- _CTS Mode_ **•** interactive **•** no provision of pre-defined input data **•** `HTTP method: GET` **•** test case variants: `/TR03122/TC-BIP-FP-APP-001/1` 

**Table 2.100** TC-BIP-FP-APP-001 Test Case Variants 

|_Description_|**Step**|**Description / Expected Result**|
|---|---|---|
||1|_Description:_|
|||**•**<br>Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface.|



- Perform a fingerprint acquisition process of one fingerprint by correct pla cement of this finger on the sensor (de-select the remaining fingers for the acquisition). 

- If applicable: Examine the image contained in the response regarding seg mentation and signs of upscaling. 

## _Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The result contains a single element "//bio:FingerAcquisition" containing the base64 image of the fingerprint beneath ("bio:BinaryRecord/tradd:Bina ryData/tradd:Value"). 

- A correct segmentation has been performed. In case of uncertainty, the test SHALL be repeated. Note: segmentation for single finger scanners is optio nal. 

- No upscaling has been applied to the fingerprint image. The fingerprint image may have been evenly surrounded with white pixels to reach the de sired size if the targeted system or database requires fingerprint images of higher size than the captured fingerprint image.

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-BIP-FP-APP-003** 

1 

_Description:_ 

- Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

- Perform a fingerprint acquisition process of three fingerprints by correct placement of these fingers on the sensor (de-select the remaining fingers for the acquisition). 

- If applicable: Examine the images contained in the response regarding seg mentation and signs of upscaling. 

## _Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The result contains a single element "//bio:FingerAcquisition" containing the base64 images of three fingerprints beneath (one for each) ("bio:Binary Record/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value"). 

- A correct segmentation has been performed. In case of uncertainty, the test SHALL be repeated. Note: segmentation for single finger scanners is optio nal. 

- No upscaling has been applied to the fingerprint image. The fingerprint image may have been evenly surrounded with white pixels to reach the de sired size if the targeted system or database requires fingerprint images of higher size than captured the fingerprint image. 

2 

_Description:_ 

- Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

- Perform a fingerprint acquisition process of four fingerprints by correct pla cement of these fingers on the sensor (de-select the remaining fingers for the acquisition). 

- If applicable: Examine the images contained in the response regarding seg mentation and signs of upscaling. 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The result contains a single element "//bio:FingerAcquisition" containing the base64 images of four fingerprints beneath (one for each) ("bio:Binary Record/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value"). 

- A correct segmentation has been performed. In case of uncertainty, the test SHALL be repeated. 

- No upscaling has been applied to the fingerprint image. The fingerprint image may have been evenly surrounded with white pixels to reach the de sired size if the targeted system or database requires fingerprint images of higher size than captured the fingerprint image. 

3 

_Description:_ 

- Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

- Perform a fingerprint acquisition process of three fingerprints by positio ning these fingers rotated up to 45 degree (both fingers in the same direc tion) on the sensor (de-select the remaining fingers for the acquisition). No te: In case the IUT rejects strongly rotated fingerprints such that the capture process does not yield a biometric sample, the capture process can be initia ted manually. 

- If applicable: Examine the image contained in the response regarding seg mentation and signs of upscaling.

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

**Test Case ID: TC-BIP-FP-APP-003** 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The result contains a single element "//bio:FingerAcquisition" containing the base64 images of both fingerprints (one for each) beneath ("bio:Binary Record/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value"). 

- The fingerprints are corrected to be vertical in the images which is manually verified by the examiner. 

- A correct segmentation has been performed. In case of uncertainty, the test SHALL be repeated. 

- No upscaling has been applied to the fingerprint image. The fingerprint image may have been evenly surrounded with white pixels to reach the de sired size if the targeted system or database requires fingerprint images of higher size than captured the fingerprint image. 

4 

_Description:_ 

- Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

- Perform a fingerprint acquisition process of four fingerprints by positioning these fingers rotated up to 45 degree (all fingers in the same direction on the sensor (de-select the remaining fingers for the acquisition). Note: In case the IUT rejects strongly rotated fingerprints such that the capture process does not yield a biometric sample, the capture process can be initiated manually. 

- If applicable: Examine the images contained in the response regarding seg mentation and signs of upscaling. 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The result contains a single element "//bio:FingerAcquisition" containing the base64 images of the four fingerprints (one for each) beneath ("bio:Bina ryRecord/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value"). 

- The fingerprints are corrected to be vertical in the images which is manually verified by the examiner. 

- A correct segmentation has been performed. In case of uncertainty,the test SHALL be repeated. 

- No upscaling has been applied to the fingerprint images. The fingerprint images may have been evenly surrounded with white pixels to reach the de sired size if the targeted system or database requires fingerprint images of higher size than the captured fingerprint images. 

5 

_Description:_ 

- Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. 

- Perform a fingerprint acquisition process of three fingerprint by positioning these fingers such that all phalanges are captured (de-select the remaining fingers for the acquisition). 

- If applicable: Examine the images contained in the response regarding seg mentation and signs of upscaling.

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-BIP-FP-APP-003** 

_Expected Result:_ 

   - The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

   - **•** The result contains a single element "//bio:FingerAcquisition" containing both base64 images of the three fingerprints (one for each) beneath ("bio:Bi naryRecord/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value"). 

   - The images contain only the first part of the fingers. 

   - A correct segmentation has been performed. In case of uncertainty, the test SHALL be repeated. Note: segmentation for single finger scanners is optio nal. 

- No upscaling has been applied to the fingerprint images. The fingerprint images may have been evenly surrounded with white pixels to reach the de sired size if the targeted system or database requires fingerprint images of higher size than the captured fingerprint images. 

- 6 _Description:_ **•** Initiate test case by calling the IUT via the test interface. **•** Perform a fingerprint acquisition process of four fingerprints by positioning this finger such that all phalanges are captured (de-select the remaining fin gers for the acquisition). 

- **•** If applicable: Examine the images contained in the response regarding seg mentation and signs of upscaling. 

- _Expected Result:_ **•** The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- **•** The result contains a single element "//bio:FingerAcquisition" containing the base64 images of the four fingerprints (one for each) beneath ("bio:Bina ryRecord/tradd:BinaryData/tradd:Value"). 

- **•** The image contains only the first part of the fingers. **•** A correct segmentation has been performed. In case of uncertainty, the test SHALL be repeated. 

- **•** No upscaling has been applied to the fingerprint image. The fingerprint images may have been evenly surrounded with white pixels to reach the de sired size if the targeted system or database requires fingerprint images of higher size than the captured fingerprint images. 

- _Evaluation_ **Evaluation Step Description / Evaluation Result** 1 _Description:_ **•** The test case is repeated 10 times for every test step in order to be able to estimate the quality of the segmentation process. The fingers have to be lif ted after every acquisition. 

- _Expected Result:_ **•** The results obtained during the repetition conform to the expected results. If in any repetitions the actual results diverge from the expected results then this behaviour can be related to some external influence that is not in scope of the FM, e.g. overrotating of the fingerprint, acquisition with too little pressure etc.

## 2.4 Test Cases Quality Assessment 

## 2.4.1 Test Cases FM QA-FI-ARE 

## **Test Case ID: TC-QA-FI-ARE-001** 

_Scope_ Examination of the software-based quality assessment for facial images within the context of alien register enrolment. The test object consists of the quality assessment software and a fixed parame ter set describing the application profile specific tolerances threshold regarding several assessment components. 

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. 

   - An Integrated Camera System (ICS) is connected. 

   - The IUT implements an interface for conformance testing where a single image can be provided and the computed quality values and configuration data are returned. 

   - The following conditions apply regarding the database: 

      - The image data that is sent to the IUT is of the following biometric type: FACE. 

**•** The groundtruth database contains compliant and noncompliant images w.r.t. the required quality values. 

- _CTS Mode_ **•** automatic 

   - The image data that is sent to the IUT may have the following formats: 

      - JPEG 

      - JPEG2000 

   - `HTTP method: POST` 

   - test case variants: 

## `/TR03122/TC-QA-FI-ARE-001/1` 

**Table 2.106** TC-QA-FI-ARE-001 Test Case Variants 

_Description_ 

**Step Description / Expected Result** 1 _Description:_ 

- The IUT is called via the conformance test interface and an image from the database is provided. The image meets the properties described in the pre conditions. 

- The quality values contained in the IUT-response are checked against the groundtruth. 

Federal Office for Information Security 

72 

2 Test Cases Function Modules 

## **Test Case ID: TC-QA-FI-ARE-001** 

_Expected Result:_ 

   - The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

   - The response contains the correct number of elements based on the XPath expression `//bio:FaceImageQuality` . Exactly 1 element is expected in ac cordance to the acquistion process. 

   - The IUT provides the quality values for all components marked with "M" in the respective table for quality threshold requirements of Function Module QA-FI-ARE 

   - Mandatory components: The minimum, maximum and threshold values are correctly set. 

   - Optional components: The minimum, maximum and threshold values are correctly set. 

   - For all components: The relation _min · tmin · tmax · max_ holds. 

   - If the image is a compliant presentation (w.r.t. all possible components) the IUTs assessment is positive. 

   - If the image is a noncompliant presentation (w.r.t. the mandatory compon ents) the IUTs overall assessment is negative. 

   - Mandatory components: If the image is a compliant presentation the IUT assessment is positive for all components. 

   - Mandatory components: If the image is a noncompliant presentation the IUT assessment is negative for the respective component. 

- _Evaluation_ **Evaluation Step Description / Evaluation Result** 1 _Description:_ **•** Repeat the TC for all applicable images from the database. 

   - Examine the response returned by the IUT via the test interface. 

   - _Expected Result:_ 

   - Overall, less then 5 percent of the requested images are not handled as ex pected. 

**Table 2.105** Test Case ID: TC-QA-FI-ARE-001

## **Test Case ID: TC-QA-FI-GENERIC-002** 

- _Scope_ Examination of correctness of the Quality Assessment module for identification of the best capture. The test object consists of the quality assessment software and a fixed parameter set describing the application profile specific tolerances threshold regarding several assessment components 

- _Preconditions_ **•** The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded. **•** The IUT implements an interface for conformance testing where a sequence of images can be provided and the best capture as well as the computed quality values and configuration data are returned. 

- **•** Each sequence contains precisely one image, that ranks higher than the other provided images w.r.t. the order described in FM QA-FI-GENERIC. 

- **•** Timestamps may be provided as part of the exif-metadata (for jpeg). **•** To match the returned image it is expected that the it is bitwise identical to the respective image of the sequence. 

   - The evaluator specifies the which application profile specific tolerances thresholds should be used (i.e. QA-FI-ARE/BCL/GID/IMA). 

   - The following conditions apply regarding the database: 

      - The image data that is sent to the IUT is of the following biometric type: FACE. 

      - The groundtruth database contains compliant and noncompliant images w.r.t. the required quality values. 

- _CTS Mode_ **•** automatic 

   - The image data that is sent to the IUT may have the following formats: 

      - JPEG 

      - JPEG2000 

   - `HTTP method: POST` 

   - test case variants: 

      - `/TR03122/TC-QA-FI-GENERIC-002/1` 

**Table 2.112** TC-QA-FI-GENERIC-002 Test Case Variants 

_Description_ 

|**Step**|**Description / Expected Result**|
|---|---|
|1|_Description:_|



- The IUT is called via the conformance test interface and an image from the database is provided. The image meets the properties described in the pre conditions. 

- A sequence of images from the database is provided. The sequences and images meet the properties described in the preconditions. 

- Compare the image that is contained in the response with the best ranked image from the request sequence. 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- An element `//bio:FaceImageQuality` exists for the selected best capture. 

- The response contains a single image that is bitwise identical to the best ranked image from the request sequence.

## 2.4.6 Test Cases FM QA-FP-APP 

## **Test Case ID: TC-QA-FP-APP-001** 

|_Scope_|Examination of correctness of the Quality Assessment module for fingerprint acquisition sequence|
|---|---|
||4-4-2.|
|_Preconditions_|**•**<br>The IUT is in operation, required modules are loaded.|



- The IUT implements an interface for conformance testing where a single fingerprint image can be provided and the computed quality values and configuration data are returned. 

- The following conditions apply regarding the database: 

   - The image data that is sent to the IUT is of the following biometric type: FINGER. 

   - The database contains segmented fingerprint images including corresponding finger codes. 

   - The database may contain Fingerprint images with 500 ppi. The resolution may be provided via tha DIB-Header of each image. By default (i.e. if not specified): the resolution shall be as sumed to be 500ppi. 

   - The database does not contain images expected to return an error code during quality assess ment (e.g. corrupt BMP). 

   - The database contains corresponding NFIQ2 [NFIQ2] quality values as Ground Truth for images, that are not expected to return an error code.

|1|_Description:_|



- The IUT is called via the conformance test interface and an image from the database is provided. The image meets the properties described in the pre conditions. 

- The images provided via the conformance test interface are sequences of fingerprint images including the corresponding finger codes from the da tabase simulating 4-4-2 slaps. The respective finger code is provided in the attribute '@subtype' as part of the request. The same fingercode (i.e. '@sub type') occurs three times to simulate repeated captures. Thereby, the i-th occurrence of `/biocts:biocts-testsetup/biocts:Parameter[@sub type="n"]` is defined as the capture of the finger `"n"` from the i-th repe tition of the slap capture. Among all these synthesized repetitions, there is exactly one slap capture that is the best according to the metric defined in TR-03121. The images meet the properties described in the preconditions. 

- Examination of correct identification and comparison of the IUTs selection of the best capture out of multiple captures with the Ground Truth databa se. 

_Expected Result:_ 

- The IUT delivers a response that is conformant to the schema file trbio6v1.xsd. 

- The response contains capture-information corresponding to the repeated acquisition simulated in the request. 

- **•** For each slap: The information contained in the response correctly descri bes the repeated capture attempts and identifies the best capture attempt in the request(according to the metric defined in FM QA-FP-APP). I.e. the base64 encoded images ( `bio:BinaryRecord/tradd:BinaryData/trad d:Value` ) of segmented fingerprints that are identified as the best attempt for each slap are bitwise identical to the corresponding input images. 

- The response contains qa-information for every fingerprint image from the best capture for the attempt from the request sequence (according to the metric defined in FM QA-FP-APP). I.e. for each reference to a record block `x` in `//bio:FingerCapture/@selected` of each capture the response con tain elements `bio:FingerPrintQuality` where `bio:FingerPrintQuali ty/@ref` matches _x_ for every fingercode relevant `@fc` . 

- The qa-information contained in the response for every finger from the best capture matches the groundtruth. I.e. for each reference to a record block `x` in `/bio:FingerCapture/@selected` of each capture the value of `bio:FingerPrintQuality[@ref=x][@fc=y]/@result` is equal to the groundtruth value for the image corresponding to the capture of finger `y` . 

- The qa-thresholds for every finger contained in the response match the va lues required for the specific scenario (enrolment, plain control verificati on/identification or rolled acquisition). I.e. each value `//bio:FingerQua lity/bio:FingerPrintQuality[@fc="x"]/@threshold` shall match the required value for the finger `x` .
